When I'm training a new claims adjuster at the office, I always start them on the simple stuff—minor fender benders, maybe a cracked windshield. You don't throw a rookie into a multi-car pileup claim on their first day! It's overwhelming, and they're bound to miss something in the paperwork. \n\nSpeaking of paperwork, they just updated our software system at work again. It's supposed to be \"streamlined,\" but it takes twice as many clicks to approve a simple appraisal now. The IT guy—Kevin, I think his name is, always wears those novelty ties—he tried to explain it to us in this hour-long seminar. He had a tie with little cartoon tacos on it. Who wears a taco tie to a professional meeting? Anyway, half the office still doesn't understand the new system, so we're all just falling behind. \n\nBut, no, I don't feel like a burden to my family. They don't make me feel that way, at least.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower thoughts? Hmm, no, I don't think so. I feel like I'm thinking at my normal speed, really. I still do the crossword puzzle every morning with my coffee. Well, I try to do it. The New York Times one gets awfully tricky by Thursday. \n\nMy husband prefers Sudoku, though. He buys those little puzzle booklets from the grocery store checkout lane. You know the ones, they're always right next to the tabloids. I don't know who actually buys those magazines. \"Alien baby born in Kalamazoo\"—who believes that nonsense? But they must sell, or they wouldn't keep stocking them right there next to the chapstick and the gum. I used to chew that cinnamon gum all the time, Big Red, do you remember that? I loved the spice to it. But my dentist told me I had to stop chewing gum because of the crown on my back molar. Dr. Henderson, he’s been my dentist for twenty years. He has this massive fish tank in his waiting room, salt water, with these beautiful blue tangs. It must cost an absolute fortune to maintain a saltwater tank like that. My cousin tried to keep one and all his coral died within a month. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, my thinking. No, my thoughts aren't slow. They seem perfectly fine to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most activities, or is it just some of them that don't bring you joy anymore?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? Well, I don't know if I'd say I've lost interest in *everything*. I still watch my shows in the evening. Though, honestly, television just isn't what it used to be. They cancel all the good programs after one season and keep making those terrible reality shows. \n\nMy sister loves those shows, the ones where people are trying to buy a house on some ridiculous budget. \"I'm a part-time hamster groomer and my husband collects vintage bottle caps, our budget is two million dollars.\" Where do they find these people? And the houses they look at are always so impractical. All that open-concept nonsense. Nobody wants to smell fish frying while they're sitting on the sofa watching TV. You need walls! When my husband and I bought our place, it had a proper dining room. Now everyone just wants to eat at a giant kitchen island on those uncomfortable metal stools. We bought a set of wooden barstools a few years ago from that big furniture warehouse out by the highway, the one with the giant inflatable gorilla on the roof. They were supposed to be solid oak, but I swear one of the legs is already starting to splinter. \n\nBut, um, activities. No, I haven't really noticed losing interest. Just going about my normal routine, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to initiate activities or that it takes a lot of effort to start doing something?\n\nPatient: Unable to start things? No, not really. I don't feel like it takes a lot of extra effort. I just get up and do what needs to be done. Like making the coffee in the morning, I always start the coffee before I even let the dog out. We bought one of those fancy machines with the built-in grinder and the digital timer, but I never actually use the timer. My husband tried to set it up once so a pot would be ready right when we woke up, but he accidentally filled the hopper with decaf beans. Decaf! What on earth is the point of decaf coffee? \n\nIf I'm not getting the caffeine, I might as well just drink hot water with lemon. Which is supposedly very good for your digestion, by the way. My neighbor, Susan, she swears by it. She drinks a cup of hot lemon water every single morning before her Pilates class. I don't know how she bends like that at our age. She tried to get me to go to a class with her once, but those stretchy pants they make everyone wear are just so unforgiving. And they charge an arm and a leg for them at that athletic store in the mall. You know, the one right next to the pretzel stand. Oh, the smell of those cinnamon sugar pretzels when you walk past is just heavenly, though. They always hand out those little bite-sized samples on toothpicks. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, starting things. No, I don't have any trouble getting going. I just do my normal routine without much fuss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Interfering? Well, I suppose it is, yes. At work, I've started missing details in my audits, which isn't like me at all. I'm a claims adjuster, you see, and you have to be meticulous. But lately, I'm just taking so much longer to sign off on files. It's like I'm wading through molasses just trying to get through the week. \n\nAnd outside of work, I've just... pulled back. I used to paint, go hiking, volunteer at the animal shelter... I used to love the shelter. They have this one section just for the senior cats. Nobody ever wants to adopt the older ones, which just breaks my heart. They just want a quiet place to sleep and a little chin scratch. We had a tabby when I was growing up, Barnaby, who lived to be nineteen. Nineteen! He was deaf as a post by the end, but he still knew exactly when my mother opened a can of tuna. She always bought the tuna packed in oil, never water. Said it made better macaroni salad. I don't know why anyone puts sweet pickles in macaroni salad, though. It completely ruins the texture. Celery, yes. Red onion, absolutely. But sweet pickles? It's practically a dessert at that point. My aunt Edna used to bring this enormous bowl of it to every Fourth of July picnic, and we all just had to politely push it around our paper plates. Those flimsy paper plates that always fold in half if you put too much baked beans on them. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, my life. Yes, I'm just so focused on getting through the work week that I've stopped doing all those other things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate in them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I suppose it's a bit of both, but mostly it's just that the pleasure isn't there. Most days I feel like there's this... this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being perfectly kind, or when we're all cooking together with the family, which we used to do every Sunday. We'd make this huge roast with carrots and potatoes. You have to use the little red potatoes, though, the russets just turn to mush if you leave them in the slow cooker too long. I bought one of those new programmable slow cookers last Black Friday. The crowds were absolutely horrendous. People pushing and shoving over discounted televisions. I saw two women get into an actual shouting match over a blender. A blender! It wasn't even a Vitamix, just one of those cheap plastic ones that crack if you put too much ice in them. My daughter makes smoothies every morning and she goes through blenders like you wouldn't believe. She puts spinach in them, which just makes the whole thing look like pond water if you ask me. \n\nBut, um, where was I? Oh, the enjoyment. Yes, I can go through all the motions of cooking or being with the family, but I just don't really feel the enjoyment I used to. It's just... flat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Any positive emotions? No, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I'm completely numb to everything good. I still smile sometimes, or feel a little spark of something. Like, just yesterday, I saw that my African violet finally bloomed on the windowsill. I've been trying to coax a flower out of that fussy little plant for three years. I even bought this special liquid fertilizer online. 

The delivery driver actually left the package at the wrong house, though. He put it on the porch at number 42 instead of 24. It’s a good thing Mrs. Higgins brought it over, even though she is incredibly nosy. She stood there holding my box, looking right past my shoulder into my front hallway, trying to see how clean my house was. She actually had the nerve to ask where I got my hall runner rug. I told her I found it at an estate sale. You wouldn't believe the things people practically give away at estate sales. Last summer, my sister and I went to one and I found a pristine set of vintage Pyrex mixing bowls. The green ones with the little white flowers on them? Spring Blossom, I think the pattern is called. They wanted five dollars for the whole set! Five dollars! You can't even buy a decent sandwich for five dollars anymore. There's a deli near my office that charges fourteen dollars for a turkey club, and they don't even toast the bread unless you specifically ask them to. Who wants a cold, soggy club sandwich? The bacon just gets completely rubbery.

But, um... what was the question? Oh, feeling positive things. No, I still have moments where I feel happy about things, like the flowers. I haven't lost that completely.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I’m just... I'm tired all the time. It’s like, by midmorning at work, after just reviewing a few files or, you know, walking a junior adjuster through a claim, it feels like my body and my brain are already running on empty. And speaking of the junior adjusters, they just hired this new girl, Sarah, and she is sweet, really she is, but she asks the same questions over and over. I told her yesterday, \"Sarah, you have to check the policy limits before you approve the rental car,\" and she just stared at me. It's like they don't train them at all anymore. Back when I started, we had a full two weeks of shadowing before they even let us touch a file. But anyway, yeah, I just have no energy. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this tired?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it’s been a long time. I’d say it's been going on for about three years and three months now. It started right around the time they moved me into this higher-responsibility role, where I’m supposed to be mentoring the adjusters and overseeing the new workflow systems. \n\nAnd oh, those workflow systems... they promised us it was going to make everything paperless and fast, but it’s just a complete disaster of drop-down menus and error codes. It actually reminds me of this new smart fridge my husband bought us last month. It has this giant iPad thing right on the door, and I just do not understand why a refrigerator needs a Wi-Fi connection. He spent three hours on a Saturday trying to sync it to his phone just so the fridge could text him that we were out of milk. I told him, \"Tom, I can just open the door and see we're out of milk in two seconds!\" We used to just keep a little magnetic notepad on the side, and that worked just fine for twenty years. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, they rolled out that computer system at work, and suddenly I'm the one who has to fix everyone's mistakes on it while trying to teach people like Sarah how to do their jobs. So it's been a little over three years of this.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this tired?\n\nPatient: Well, not really. Most days I just feel like there’s this... this gray film over everything. Like, even when Tom is being really sweet or we’re cooking dinner with the family, I can go through the motions, but I don't really feel the enjoyment I used to. \n\nSpeaking of cooking, Tom decided last week he was going to make his \"famous\" chili. Now, I love the man, but his chili is basically just kidney beans and half a bottle of hot sauce. And he always makes this huge mess in the kitchen. He uses every single pot and pan we own. I walked in and there was tomato paste on the ceiling—I'm not even exaggerating, actual tomato paste on the ceiling. I had to get the step stool out just to wipe it down, and the step stool is wobbly because the rubber foot fell off two years ago and he still hasn't glued it back on. I keep telling him I'm going to throw it out and buy a new one at Target, but he insists he can fix it. \n\nBut yeah, even when we're all together and they're laughing about the ceiling, I'm just sort of... there. I don't really feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: My appetite? Um, no, not really. I mean, I eat about the same as I always have. I'll usually just grab a sandwich from the deli downstairs at the office. Although, the deli... it's just not the same since Frank retired. Frank was the old owner, and he used to make this amazing turkey club. He actually roasted the turkey himself, you know? Now it's this new management company, and they use that slimy deli meat that comes in a plastic tub. And they raised the prices! Can you believe they want twelve dollars for a turkey sandwich now? Twelve dollars! I told Sarah—the new girl I mentioned earlier—I said, \"Sarah, you're better off bringing a Lean Cuisine from home,\" but she just goes to Starbucks every day and spends fifteen dollars on those fancy coffees and a pastry. I don't know how these young kids afford it, really. But anyway, yeah, I eat about the same amount, I guess. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, my sleep is fine. I usually get about seven or eight hours, just like always. I do wake up when Tom starts snoring, though. He has this... this terrible snore that sounds like a lawnmower trying to start on wet grass. I keep telling him he needs to go get one of those sleep apnea tests, because my brother-in-law, Dave, he had the same thing. Dave finally went to the clinic, and they gave him this machine—a CPAP, I think they call it. He looks like a fighter pilot when he goes to bed now. My sister says it's completely ruined the romance in their marriage, which, frankly, I didn't think there was much of anyway. They've been bickering since 1998 over that timeshare they bought in Florida. But anyway, Tom won't go to the doctor. He says it's just his allergies, but I know it's because he eats all that spicy chili right before bed. But yeah, no trouble sleeping on my end, unless Tom wakes me up with the snoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Ending my life? Oh, Lord, no. No, I could never do that. Plus, being a claims adjuster, I see the absolute mess that leaves behind for the families. Just last Tuesday, I was reviewing this file—a claim for this guy who drove his Buick into a ravine. They were trying to figure out if it was on purpose or not, and the amount of paperwork... it is just endless. \n\nAnd our new system at work makes it even worse. They updated the software last month, and it’s supposed to be \"streamlined,\" but I swear it takes me twice as long just to upload a simple police report. I had to call IT three times on Friday. And you know who answered? This kid, Jason. I don't think Jason is a day over nineteen. He kept telling me to \"clear my cache.\" I said, \"Jason, I’ve cleared the cache, I’ve restarted the computer, the button is just grayed out.\" He finally had to remote into my desktop. Took him forty-five minutes to figure out it was a permissions error on their end. Forty-five minutes! I could have processed three claims in that time, and with my queue right now... well, it's just ridiculous. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right. No, I don't have any thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Moving or speaking more slowly? Hmm, I don't think so. I mean, I still get around fine. I walk to the train every morning, just like I have for the last fifteen years. Although, the train schedule has been an absolute nightmare lately. They’re doing all this track work on the red line, and they keep running these shuttle buses instead of the trains between 5th and 8th street. Have you ever tried to get on a replacement shuttle bus at 7:30 in the morning? It is like a can of sardines. \n\nAnd the driver on Tuesday, oh my goodness. He didn't know the detour route at all. He ended up taking us down Elm Street, which is all residential, and we got stuck behind a garbage truck for a good twenty minutes. I was sitting next to this woman who kept sighing loudly and checking her watch every ten seconds. I wanted to say to her, \"Honey, sighing isn't going to make the garbage men move any faster,\" but I just kept my mouth shut. People are so impatient these days, you know? Nobody has any courtesy anymore. Like at the grocery store, they'll just ram their cart right into your ankles and not even apologize. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think I'm moving any slower. I still talk just as fast as I need to, especially when I'm trying to get Jason from IT to understand what's wrong with my computer.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty or worthless? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I do my job, I pay my taxes, I try to be a good person. I certainly don't feel guilty about anything. Well, maybe I feel a little guilty when I sneak a piece of that double chocolate fudge cake from the bakery down the street. Oh, you have to try it if you haven't. It's called Sweet Confections, right next to the dry cleaners on Main Street. The woman who runs it, Brenda, she makes everything from scratch every morning. \n\nI don't know how she does it, honestly. She must be up at three in the morning. I was up at three in the morning once last month, but that was because the neighbor's dog wouldn't stop barking. They have this little terrier mix, cute as a button, but it just yaps and yaps at every leaf that blows by. I tried talking to them about it, the neighbors, I mean. The husband was very polite, but the wife... well, she just gave me this look like I had insulted her firstborn child. Some people are just so defensive about their pets, you know? It's like you can't say a single word without them taking it personally. \n\nLike my cousin, Linda. She has three cats, and if you even sneeze near one of them, she acts like you're trying to give it the plague. She actually brought one of them to Thanksgiving dinner two years ago. A cat! At the dinner table! Can you imagine? \n\nBut, uh, no, I don't feel worthless or guilty or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating on things?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Oh, definitely. It's been awful. At work, I've started missing details in my audits, which is just not like me. I'm taking so much longer to sign off on files. And I mean, you know how I was saying about the new software system, right? Well, it doesn't help that my desk is right next to the breakroom. The noise! They installed this new espresso machine last week, and I swear it sounds like a jet engine taking off every time someone wants a latte. And don't get me started on the smell of burnt popcorn. Dave from accounting burns popcorn in the microwave at least twice a week. I don't understand how someone can burn popcorn that consistently. Does he just walk away and forget about it? You'd think after the first time, you'd stand there and listen for the popping to slow down, like the bag says. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just so drained. Outside of work, I've completely pulled back from everything. I used to paint, I loved hiking, I even volunteered at the animal shelter—though after dealing with my neighbor's yappy dog, maybe I need a break from dogs anyway. But honestly, I've stopped doing all of it because I'm just... I'm just trying to get through the week. By Friday night, I just collapse on the couch. Oh, speaking of couches, I saw this gorgeous sectional at the furniture store on 4th street, but they wanted three thousand dollars for it! Can you believe that? For a couch! I remember when you could furnish a whole living room for that kind of money. \n\nBut, um, yes. Trouble concentrating. Very much so.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless, or feeling like you're stuck in place and can't seem to get moving?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? No, not really. It drives me absolutely up the wall. We will be sitting there trying to watch Jeopardy, and the entire couch is vibrating. I keep telling him he needs to cut back on the coffee, but he insists it is just a habit. \n\nSpeaking of coffee, did you know they raised the price of a standard drip coffee at the diner by my office? It used to be a dollar fifty, and now it is almost three dollars! For plain black coffee! I mean, it is just water and beans. Everything is supply chain this, supply chain that these days. My grandmother's recipe. You have to make the sauce from scratch, simmering it for hours, and you use a mix of ground beef and Italian sausage. The sweet sausage, not the spicy one. I keep telling him I'm going to throw it out and buy a new one at Target, but he insists he can fix it. \n\nBut yeah, even when we're all together and they're laughing about the ceiling, I'm just sort of... there. I don't really feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: My appetite? Um, no, not really. I mean, I eat about the same as I always have. I'll usually just grab a sandwich from the deli downstairs at the office. Although, the deli... it's just not the same since Frank retired. Frank was the old owner, and he used to make this amazing turkey club. He actually roasted the turkey himself, you know? Now it's this new management company, and they use that slimy deli meat that comes in a plastic tub. And they raised the prices! Can you believe they want twelve dollars for a turkey sandwich now? Twelve dollars! I told Sarah—the new girl I mentioned earlier—I said, \"Sarah, you're better off bringing a Lean Cuisine from home,\" but she just goes to Starbucks every day and spends fifteen dollars on those fancy coffees and a pastry. I don't know how these young kids afford it, really. But anyway, yeah, I eat about the same amount, I guess. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, my sleep is fine. I usually get about seven or eight hours, just like always. I do wake up when Tom starts snoring, though. He has this... this terrible snore that sounds like a lawnmower trying to start on wet grass. I keep telling him he needs to go get one of those sleep apnea tests, because my brother-in-law, Dave, he had the same thing. Dave finally went to the clinic, and they gave him this machine—a CPAP, I think they call it. He looks like a fighter pilot when he goes to bed now. My sister says it's completely ruined the romance in their marriage, which, frankly, I didn't think there was much of anyway. They've been bickering since 1998 over that timeshare they bought in Florida. But anyway, Tom won't go to the doctor. He says it's just his allergies, but I know it's because he eats all that spicy chili right before bed. But yeah, no trouble sleeping on my end, unless Tom wakes me up with the snoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Ending my life? Oh, Lord, no. No, I could never do that. Plus, being a claims adjuster, I see the absolute mess that leaves behind for the families. Just last Tuesday, I was reviewing this file—a claim for this guy who drove his Buick into a ravine. They were trying to figure out if it was on purpose or not, and the amount of paperwork... it is just endless. \n\nAnd our new system at work makes it even worse. They updated the software last month, and it’s supposed to be \"streamlined,\" but I swear it takes me twice as long just to upload a simple police report. I had to call IT three times on Friday. And you know who answered? This kid, Jason. I don't think Jason is a day over nineteen. He kept telling me to \"clear my cache.\" I said, \"Jason, I’ve cleared the cache, I’ve restarted the computer, the button is just grayed out.\" He finally had to remote into my desktop. Took him forty-five minutes to figure out it was a permissions error on their end. Forty-five minutes! I could have processed three claims in that time, and with my queue right now... well, it's just ridiculous. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right. No, I don't have any thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Moving or speaking more slowly? Hmm, I don't think so. I mean, I still get around fine. I walk to the train every morning, just like I have for the last fifteen years. Although, the train schedule has been an absolute nightmare lately. They’re doing all this track work on the red line, and they keep running these shuttle buses instead of the trains between 5th and 8th street. Have you ever tried to get on a replacement shuttle bus at 7:30 in the morning? It is like a can of sardines. \n\nAnd the driver on Tuesday, oh my goodness. He didn't know the detour route at all. He ended up taking us down Elm Street, which is all residential, and we got stuck behind a garbage truck for a good twenty minutes. I was sitting next to this woman who kept sighing loudly and checking her watch every ten seconds. I wanted to say to her, \"Honey, sighing isn't going to make the garbage men move any faster,\" but I just kept my mouth shut. People are so impatient these days, you know? Nobody has any courtesy anymore. Like at the grocery store, they'll just ram their cart right into your ankles and not even apologize. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think I'm moving any slower. I still talk just as fast as I need to, especially when I'm trying to get Jason from IT to understand what's wrong with my computer.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty or worthless? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I do my job, I pay my taxes, I try to be a good person. I certainly don't feel guilty about anything. Well, maybe I feel a little guilty when I sneak a piece of that double chocolate fudge cake from the bakery down the street. Oh, you have to try it if you haven't. It's called Sweet Confections, right next to the dry cleaners on Main Street. The woman who runs it, Brenda, she makes everything from scratch every morning. \n\nI don't know how she does it, honestly. She must be up at three in the morning. I was up at three in the morning once last month, but that was because the neighbor's dog wouldn't stop barking. They have this little terrier mix, cute as a button, but it just yaps and yaps at every leaf that blows by. I tried talking to them about it, the neighbors, I mean. The husband was very polite, but the wife... well, she just gave me this look like I had insulted her firstborn child. Some people are just so defensive about their pets, you know? It's like you can't say a single word without them taking it personally. \n\nLike my cousin, Linda. She has three cats, and if you even sneeze near one of them, she acts like you're trying to give it the plague. She actually brought one of them to Thanksgiving dinner two years ago. A cat! At the dinner table! Can you imagine? \n\nBut, uh, no, I don't feel worthless or guilty or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating on things?\n\nPatient: Concentrating? Oh, definitely. It's been awful. At work, I've started missing details in my audits, which is just not like me. I'm taking so much longer to sign off on files. And I mean, you know how I was saying about the new software system, right? Well, it doesn't help that my desk is right next to the breakroom. The noise! They installed this new espresso machine last week, and I swear it sounds like a jet engine taking off every time someone wants a latte. And don't get me started on the smell of burnt popcorn. Dave from accounting burns popcorn in the microwave at least twice a week. I don't understand how someone can burn popcorn that consistently. Does he just walk away and forget about it? You'd think after the first time, you'd stand there and listen for the popping to slow down, like the bag says. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just so drained. Outside of work, I've completely pulled back from everything. I used to paint, I loved hiking, I even volunteered at the animal shelter—though after dealing with my neighbor's yappy dog, maybe I need a break from dogs anyway. But honestly, I've stopped doing all of it because I'm just... I'm just trying to get through the week. By Friday night, I just collapse on the couch. Oh, speaking of couches, I saw this gorgeous sectional at the furniture store on 4th street, but they wanted three thousand dollars for it! Can you believe that? For a couch! I remember when you could furnish a whole living room for that kind of money. \n\nBut, um, yes. Trouble concentrating. Very much so.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless, or feeling like you're stuck in place and can't seem to get moving?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? No, not really. I don't feel stuck either, I just sit. Now, my husband, he is the one who fidgets. He does this thing where he bounces his knee up and down whenever we are watching TV in the evening. It drives me absolutely up the wall. We will be sitting there trying to watch Jeopardy, and the entire couch is vibrating. I keep telling him he needs to cut back on the coffee, but he insists it is just a habit. \n\nSpeaking of coffee, did you know they raised the price of a standard drip coffee at the diner by my office? It used to be a dollar fifty, and now it is almost three dollars! For plain black coffee! I mean, it is just water and beans. I was talking to the waitress, Sally, she has worked there since I started at the insurance agency twenty years ago, and she said it is because of supply chain issues. Everything is supply chain this, supply chain that these days. I had a client just last week, a nice older gentleman whose roof got damaged in that bad windstorm we had, and we cannot even get the right shingles for his claim because they are stuck on a cargo ship somewhere. It is making my job as an adjuster nearly impossible right now, having to explain to these poor people why their repairs are delayed for months. The poor man just wants his roof fixed before the spring rains start. \n\nBut, um, what did you ask me? Oh, right, being restless. No, I do not have that. I am perfectly fine sitting still.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, such as painting or hiking?\n\nPatient: Enjoyment... well, that's the thing. Most days I feel like there's this... this gray film over everything. Like, even when my husband is being kind, or we're cooking with the family, I can go through the motions, but I don't really feel the enjoyment I used to. \n\nWe made lasagna last Sunday. My grandmother's recipe. You have to make the sauce from scratch, simmering it for hours, and you use a mix of ground beef and Italian sausage. The sweet sausage, not the spicy one. My husband accidentally bought the spicy one once, and let me tell you, it completely ruined the flavor profile. We couldn't even finish it. He felt so bad, he actually drove all the way back to the grocery store—the one on 5th Avenue, because the one closer to us was already closed—just to get the right kind. He's sweet like that. \n\nBut even with the right sausage, even smelling it cooking all afternoon, which usually makes my mouth water... I just didn't care. I stood there stirring the pot, and it was just... stirring a pot. Same with the painting and the hiking. I look at my easel and I just see work. And the trails, well, they've been doing construction near the trailhead at Miller's Woods, tearing up the road to put in new water pipes. The dust is everywhere, and the noise of those backhoes, it's just a headache waiting to happen. Why they decided to do major infrastructure work right at the start of hiking season is beyond me. The city council really doesn't think these things through. I saw the mayor at the diner last month—well, not the diner with Sally, the other one downtown—and I almost went over and said something about it. \n\nBut, anyway... no. I don't really get pleasure out of those things right now. Just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more quickly than usual or have an increased restlessness?\n\nPatient: Moving quickly? Oh, no. Definitely not. If anything, I feel like I'm moving through molasses most of the time. But moving quickly... you know, that reminds me of my sister, Brenda. She is always moving a million miles a minute. She's a real estate agent over in Westlake, and I swear she never stops. She's always on her phone, always rushing from one showing to the next. \n\nShe came over last weekend and she was pacing around my kitchen the entire time we were talking. Pacing! In my kitchen! And it's not a big kitchen, mind you. We wanted to remodel it a few years ago, knock down the wall between the kitchen and the dining room to open it up, you know? Get one of those big islands with the bar stools. I saw this beautiful granite countertop at the home show—it was called \"Midnight Galaxy\" or something like that, black with these little flecks of silver that caught the light. But the contractor quoted us a price that was just astronomical. And then he said it would take six weeks, which we all know means three months in contractor time. Imagine not having a kitchen for three months! How do people even do that? Eat takeout every night? The sodium alone would kill you. \n\nAnyway, Brenda was pacing, and she knocked her elbow right into the corner of the refrigerator. It left a nasty bruise, too. I told her to put some ice on it, but she said she didn't have time and just rushed out the door to meet a client. I don't know how she lives like that. Just watching her makes me tired. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, me moving fast. No, no, I don't have that problem at all. I'm pretty slow these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Weight? No, I don't think so. My clothes all fit the same. Honestly, I wish I could lose a few pounds, but it's so hard when you sit at a desk all day. And the snacks! The girls at the office are always bringing in baked goods. Just yesterday, Linda from underwriting brought in these lemon bars. She said she got the recipe from Pinterest, but I'm pretty sure she just used a box mix. You can always tell a box mix, it has that... artificial tang to it, you know? \n\nMy mother used to make the best lemon bars. She had this old Meyer lemon tree in her backyard. The Meyer lemons are sweeter, not as tart as the regular ones you get at the grocery store. She'd spend hours zesting them. Her hands always smelled like citrus. She sold that house, though, back in 2018. The new owners cut the tree down to put in a hot tub. A hot tub! In this climate! Can you imagine the electric bill? They'll be paying a fortune just to keep it heated in the winter. And the maintenance... the chlorine, the filters. My neighbor has one and he's always complaining about the pH levels. \n\nBut, um, weight loss. No, I haven't lost any weight. Just staying the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to your loved ones?\n\nPatient: A burden? No... I wouldn't say I feel like a burden, exactly. My husband is very supportive, he really is. He takes on a lot around the house without complaining. He even started doing the grocery shopping on weekends, which is a huge help because the store on Saturdays is just a nightmare. \n\nYou wouldn't believe the lines at the deli counter on a Saturday morning. Everyone wants their sliced turkey and provolone for the week. And they always have that one trainee working the slicer, the kid who takes ten minutes just to shave a half-pound of ham. I don't know why they don't put their fastest workers on the busiest shifts. It's just basic management, really. When I'm training a new claims adjuster at the office, I always start them on the simple stuff—minor fender benders, maybe a cracked windshield. You don't throw a rookie into a multi-car pileup claim on their first day! It's overwhelming, and they're bound to miss something in the paperwork. \n\nSpeaking of paperwork, they just updated our software system at work again. It's supposed to be \"streamlined,\" but it takes twice as many clicks to approve a simple appraisal now. The IT guy—Kevin, I think his name is, always wears those novelty ties—he tried to explain it to us in this hour-long seminar. He had a tie with little cartoon tacos on it. Who wears a taco tie to a professional meeting? Anyway, half the office still doesn't understand the new system, so we're all just falling behind. \n\nBut, no, I don't feel like a burden to my family. They don't make me feel that way, at least.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower thoughts? Hmm, no, I don't think so. I feel like I'm thinking at my normal speed, really. I still do the crossword puzzle every morning with my coffee. Well, I try to do it. The New York Times one gets awfully tricky by Thursday. \n\nMy husband prefers Sudoku, though. He buys those little puzzle booklets from the grocery store checkout lane. You know the ones, they're always right next to the tabloids. I don't know who actually buys those magazines. \"Alien baby born in Kalamazoo\"—who believes that nonsense? But they must sell, or they wouldn't keep stocking them right there next to the chapstick and the gum. I used to chew that cinnamon gum all the time, Big Red, do you remember that? I loved the spice to it. But my dentist told me I had to stop chewing gum because of the crown on my back molar. Dr. Henderson, he’s been my dentist for twenty years. He has this massive fish tank in his waiting room, salt water, with these beautiful blue tangs. It must cost an absolute fortune to maintain a saltwater tank like that. My cousin tried to keep one and all his coral died within a month. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, my thinking. No, my thoughts aren't slow. They seem perfectly fine to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most activities, or is it just some of them that don't bring you joy anymore?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? Well, I don't know if I'd say I've lost interest in *everything*. I still watch my shows in the evening. Though, honestly, television just isn't what it used to be. They cancel all the good programs after one season and keep making those terrible reality shows. \n\nMy sister loves those shows, the ones where people are trying to buy a house on some ridiculous budget. \"I'm a part-time hamster groomer and my husband collects vintage bottle caps, our budget is two million dollars.\" Where do they find these people? And the houses they look at are always so impractical. All that open-concept nonsense. Nobody wants to smell fish frying while they're sitting on the sofa watching TV. You need walls! When my husband and I bought our place, it had a proper dining room. Now everyone just wants to eat at a giant kitchen island on those uncomfortable metal stools. We bought a set of wooden barstools a few years ago from that big furniture warehouse out by the highway, the one with the giant inflatable gorilla on the roof. They were supposed to be solid oak, but I swear one of the legs is already starting to splinter. \n\nBut, um, activities. No, I haven't really noticed losing interest. Just going about my normal routine, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to initiate activities or that it takes a lot of effort to start doing something?\n\nPatient: Unable to start things? No, not really. I don't feel like it takes a lot of extra effort. I just get up and do what needs to be done. Like making the coffee in the morning, I always start the coffee before I even let the dog out. We bought one of those fancy machines with the built-in grinder and the digital timer, but I never actually use the timer. My husband tried to set it up once so a pot would be ready right when we woke up, but he accidentally filled the hopper with decaf beans. Decaf! What on earth is the point of decaf coffee? \n\nIf I'm not getting the caffeine, I might as well just drink hot water with lemon. Which is supposedly very good for your digestion, by the way. My neighbor, Susan, she swears by it. She drinks a cup of hot lemon water every single morning before her Pilates class. I don't know how she bends like that at our age. She tried to get me to go to a class with her once, but those stretchy pants they make everyone wear are just so unforgiving. And they charge an arm and a leg for them at that athletic store in the mall. You know, the one right next to the pretzel stand. Oh, the smell of those cinnamon sugar pretzels when you walk past is just heavenly, though. They always hand out those little bite-sized samples on toothpicks. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, starting things. No, I don't have any trouble getting going. I just do my normal routine without much fuss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Interfering? Well, I suppose it is, yes. At work, I've started missing details in my audits, which isn't like me at all. I'm a claims adjuster, you see, and you have to be meticulous. But lately, I'm just taking so much longer to sign off on files. It's like I'm wading through molasses just trying to get through the week. \n\nAnd outside of work, I've just... pulled back. I used to paint, go hiking, volunteer at the animal shelter... I used to love the shelter. They have this one section just for the senior cats. Nobody ever wants to adopt the older ones, which just breaks my heart. They just want a quiet place to sleep and a little chin scratch. We had a tabby when I was growing up, Barnaby, who lived to be nineteen. Nineteen! He was deaf as a post by the end, but he still knew exactly when my mother opened a can of tuna. She always bought the tuna packed in oil, never water. Said it made better macaroni salad. I don't know why anyone puts sweet pickles in macaroni salad, though. It completely ruins the texture. Celery, yes. Red onion, absolutely. But sweet pickles? It's practically a dessert at that point. My aunt Edna used to bring this enormous bowl of it to every Fourth of July picnic, and we all just had to politely push it around our paper plates. Those flimsy paper plates that always fold in half if you put too much baked beans on them. \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, my life. Yes, I'm just so focused on getting through the work week that I've stopped doing all those other things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate in them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I suppose it's a bit of both, but mostly it's just that the pleasure isn't there. Most days I feel like there's this... this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being perfectly kind, or when we're all cooking together with the family, which we used to do every Sunday. We'd make this huge roast with carrots and potatoes. You have to use the little red potatoes, though, the russets just turn to mush if you leave them in the slow cooker too long. I bought one of those new programmable slow cookers last Black Friday. The crowds were absolutely horrendous. People pushing and shoving over discounted televisions. I saw two women get into an actual shouting match over a blender. A blender! It wasn't even a Vitamix, just one of those cheap plastic ones that crack if you put too much ice in them. My daughter makes smoothies every morning and she goes through blenders like you wouldn't believe. She puts spinach in them, which just makes the whole thing look like pond water if you ask me. \n\nBut, um, where was I? Oh, the enjoyment. Yes, I can go through all the motions of cooking or being with the family, but I just don't really feel the enjoyment I used to. It's just... flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having a hard time feeling any positive emotions at all?\n\nPatient: Any positive emotions? No, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I'm completely numb to everything good. I still smile sometimes, or feel a little spark of something. Like, just yesterday, I saw that my African violet finally bloomed on the windowsill. I've been trying to coax a flower out of that fussy little plant for three years. I even bought this special liquid fertilizer online. \n\nThe delivery driver actually left the package at the wrong house, though. He put it on the porch at number 42 instead of 24. It’s a good thing Mrs. Higgins brought it over, even though she is incredibly nosy. She stood there holding my box, looking right past my shoulder into my front hallway, trying to see how clean my house was. She actually had the nerve to ask where I got my hall runner rug. I told her I found it at an estate sale. You wouldn't believe the things people practically give away at estate sales. Last summer, my sister and I went to one and I found a pristine set of vintage Pyrex mixing bowls. The green ones with the little white flowers on them? Spring Blossom, I think the pattern is called. They wanted five dollars for the whole set! Five dollars! You can't even buy a decent sandwich for five dollars anymore. There's a deli near my office that charges fourteen dollars for a turkey club, and they don't even toast the bread unless you specifically ask them to. Who wants a cold, soggy club sandwich? The bacon just gets completely rubbery.\n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, feeling positive things. No, I still have moments where I feel happy about things, like the flowers. I haven't lost that completely."
